# RustyPresenter — shared project plan

**Status:** Building. The user approved the design and asked for the app to be built on October 29, 2026. v0.2 (Tauri) is in `src/` and `web/`; the native Slint app is being built beside it (Plans 1 and 2 done on Windows, Plan 3 next). The earlier experiment is in `experiment-archive/`.
**Updated:** November 6, 2026, cloud session (one line of work: `finish` — every Nov 6 feature branch merged and tested; see the board and the newest handoff)
**Collaborators:** User, Codex, and Claude
**Purpose:** A Rust-based presentation application for this church's personal use during live services.

## Start here — for Codex and Claude

Read this file before working. It is the shared record of requirements, decisions, work ownership, and handoffs. The existing code is an unfinished experiment, not an approved architecture or a usable release.

- Preserve the user's requirements. Do not quietly replace a required integration with a lesser workflow.
- Keep planning separate from implementation. The user explicitly clarified that we are currently planning.
- Before starting an authorized task, record its owner and the files or area involved in the work board below. Read the other collaborator's latest handoff first.
- Avoid simultaneous edits to the same files. Agree on shared interfaces before implementing connected components.
- Record discoveries, decisions, checks performed, and unresolved issues here. Distinguish working functionality from designs, placeholders, and untested code.
- Review each other's work. A collaborator's completion claim alone is not release verification.
- Do not put account passwords, access tokens, API keys, or downloaded private church content in this plan or source control.
- Only the user can change product priorities. Both collaborators may propose changes and explain tradeoffs.

No tasks have been dispatched to Claude through this plan. The ownership split below is a proposal for coordination.

## 0. What controls v1

**`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-rustypresenter-v1-design.md` controls v1 behavior.** This plan holds the long-term requirements, collaboration rules, feasibility evidence, and the work board. If this plan and the spec disagree about v1, the spec wins; fix the plan.

User decisions (October 29, 2026 design review):

- Church currently uses **EasyWorship**, not ProPresenter. Goal is to save money.
- ~~v1 is Windows only~~ → **Superseded October 29–30:** the user asked for a **native app on Windows, macOS and Linux**, no browser.
- ~~Architecture: Tauri 2~~ → **Superseded:** native **Slint** UI (user holds the Slint Royalty-Free License v2.0), video through **libmpv**, PowerPoint import through PowerPoint on Windows or LibreOffice everywhere. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-native-slint-design.md`, which overrides the v1 spec's platform sections. The Tauri app keeps working until the native app reaches parity.
- **Phone remote: browser page** for v1.
- Displays: operator laptop screen, room output, optional **foldback** monitor.
- Backgrounds: color, image, looping video. **Video items with audio**, still-image items, and **audio items**.
- **Clear All / Slide / Media / Audio + Logo**, defined by the spec's clear matrix. No Blackout/Restore.
- **Next on the last slide takes the next service item live.**
- **Songs in v1:** SongSelect `.txt` import + one-time EasyWorship library import (songs only). Direct SongSelect login is deferred, not dropped.
- ~~**Bible:** all six translations required for v1 release~~ → **Changed November 6, 2026:** the user said the six names were only examples. v1 ships with KJV built in, free eBible downloads, imported licensed files and online sources with a key; no specific translation gates release.
- **Decks:** PowerPoint and Google Slides as slide images; PowerPoint renders via the installed PowerPoint.

Long-term goals stay goals. Deferring them in v1 does not remove them.

## 2. Live-service experience

Detailed v1 behavior is in the spec: layers and clears (§6), navigation and keyboard focus (§7), Bible (§8), songs and imports (§9), displays (§10), remote (§12). Summary:

1. Build the service plan from songs, scripture, decks, media, and announcements.
2. Selecting an item loads **Preview** only.
3. **Take Live** sends it to the room. Within the live item, slide clicks and arrows change the live slide; Next at the end takes the next service item.
4. **Clear All / Slide / Media / Audio / Logo** per the clear matrix.
5. Edits and imports never change the committed room output. A failure while *preparing* new content keeps the committed output. Media that fails *while playing* is cleared from its layer. A crashed room window recovers per spec §4.2 (static layers return, audible media does not).

## 4. Architecture

**Selected (October 29–30):** native Slint app, one process plus a small supervisor for crash recovery. Rust core unchanged (`model`, `live`, `bible`, `songselect`, `slides`, `store`); `App` and its dispatcher live in `src/app.rs` with no axum or Tauri types; one typed `Update` stream feeds the native UI and the phone remote's SSE; slides are drawn by the Rust compositor in `src/render/` (cosmic-text + tiny-skia, golden-image tests); video and audio through libmpv loaded at runtime; the phone remote stays a web page served by `server.rs`. Full design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-native-slint-design.md`.

Plans: Plan 1 foundation (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-29-native-foundation.md`, done); Plan 2 video, room output, stage (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-01-native-video-room-stage.md`, done on Windows); Plan 3 operator window and dialogs (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-01-native-operator.md`); Plan 4 imports, crash recovery, packaging, Tauri removal (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-01-native-imports-recovery-packaging.md`).

*Previous:* Tauri 2 with WebView2 windows (v0.2, still the working app until the native one replaces it).

## 5. Delivery sequence (v1)

Follows spec §15:

0. Setup (git, move off OneDrive, Tauri shell, audit experiment) + F1, F2, F6; F3–F5 in parallel.
1. Live core.
2. Songs (TXT, EasyWorship after F4).
3. Bible (KJV first; release gate after F5).
4. Phone remote + pairing security.
5. Decks (PowerPoint, then Google after F3).
6. Acceptance matrix + rehearsal.

No implementation starts until the user approves the written spec and the implementation plan.

## 6. Acceptance

v1 acceptance is the runtime matrix in spec §14.2 plus the core unit tests in §14.1. Long-term additions: macOS/Linux builds with multi-display checks; direct SongSelect login retrieving a song with metadata.

This is not yet ready to replace the church's existing presentation setup.

## 9. Current workspace handoff

Codex began experimenting before the user clarified that this conversation was planning only. The files remain in place for inspection, but implementation has stopped.

- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock`: Rust package with Axum, Tokio, Serde, UUID, dirs.
- `src/model.rs`: service/slide structures, live snapshots, commands, JSON persistence.
- `src/server.rs`: local HTTP host, auth, pairing, commands, TXT conversion, KJV endpoints.
- `src/main.rs`: experimental host that opens a browser operator UI.
- `src/songselect.rs`: lyric TXT parser and slide splitting. Check its footer detection against the writers-before-`CCLI Song #` order.
- `src/bible.rs`: experimental KJV parser. **Known failing tests** (27,691 of 31,102 verses; legacy book IDs such as `JOH`, `1JO`, `EZE`, `MAR` unmapped; audit every mapping; source includes deuterocanon).
- `web/`: unfinished operator, remote, output pages.
- `assets/bibles/`: KJV source text and source info.
- `.tools/`, `target/`: local toolchain and build output; git-ignored.
- Last recorded: Rust compiled on Windows (GNU); tests 9 passed, 3 failed (all KJV). No end-to-end, phone, projector, macOS, or Linux verification.

The experiment predates the Tauri decision. Do not build on it without AUDIT-01.

## 10. Open product decisions

Resolved on October 29: displays (room + optional foldback), remote form (browser), media scope (backgrounds, video with audio, images, audio items), existing material (EasyWorship songs only), deck fidelity (images first), navigation at item end, Bible release gate.

Still open:

1. CCLI partner access for direct login (long-term).
2. Bible source choices and costs (F5); any translation exception is the user's decision.
3. Service planning integration (e.g., Planning Center): not requested; local service plans only for v1. Now part of FEAT-7 (§10b).

## 10b. Feature roadmap (after native parity)

**User decision, November 1, 2026:** the user wants the missing features from both EasyWorship and ProPresenter ("we want it all") and approved this breakdown and order. Each project gets its own brainstorm → written spec → plan → build, after Plans 3 and 4. Earlier projects come first because later ones depend on them.

| ID | Project | Contains | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| FEAT-1 | **Looks and themes** | Theme per song/item (not one global theme); formatting on text slides (bold, colour, size); background library with thumbnails; props (overlays that stay up while slides change, e.g. a corner logo); looks (which layers each screen shows) | Plan 3 |
| FEAT-2 | **Service automation** | Timers (count down to a time of day, count down a length, count up) shown in the room and/or on stage; messages with fill-in fields (nursery alerts); macros (one button runs several steps); slide timers and announcement loops; pre-service countdown | FEAT-1 |
| FEAT-3 | **Stage display** | Foldback layouts; timers, notes, current and next slide on stage; stage message box | FEAT-2 |
| FEAT-4 | **Song workflow** | Coloured song sections with a jump key per section (e.g. `C` for chorus); editing a song while it is live; arrangement drag-to-reorder | Plan 3 (can move earlier) |
| FEAT-5 | **Bible extras** | Keyword search; two translations side by side; choosing where a passage breaks across slides. Translation licensing (F5) continues alongside | Plan 3 |
| FEAT-6 | **Service planning** | Service templates and "copy last week"; print/export the service order; practice mode (run a service without the room screen) | Plan 3 |
| FEAT-7 | **Livestream and reporting** | Livestream output with lower-thirds (uses FEAT-1 looks); CCLI usage reports; Planning Center | FEAT-1 |

Left out unless the user asks: live camera input, recording or streaming from the app, MIDI, DMX lighting, timecode, custom clear groups.

Plan 3 stays a 1:1 port of the web operator. Lyric search already exists there (the Add dialog search matches any field, lyrics included), so only the FEAT-4 items are new.

## 10c. Ideas from the ProPresenter 7 beginner's guide (user approved all, November 6, 2026)

Source notes: `docs/research/2026-11-06-propresenter7-beginners-guide-notes.md`. The user asked for every idea to go into the plan. Each one is listed under the §10b project it belongs to. Items marked *new* were not in §10b before.

| # | Idea | Goes in | What "done" looks like |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Layers with their own clear buttons (Background, Text, Announcements, Messages, Audio); a button lights up while its layer is showing | FEAT-1 | Clearing one layer leaves the others on screen; the lit buttons always match the room screen |
| 2 | Every song gets an Intro slide (holds the background and actions) and a Blank slide | FEAT-4 | New songs and EasyWorship imports get both; the Blank slide shows no lyrics |
| 3 | Arrangements: a song is made of sections; an arrangement is an ordered list of them; several per song | FEAT-4 | Editing a section once changes every repeat of it |
| 4 | Whole-song text editor: Enter = new line, a shortcut = new slide, Backspace at a slide's start merges it with the one before | FEAT-4 | A song can be re-split without opening each slide |
| 5 | Import dialog: lines per slide, break on line or paragraph, theme applied on import; paste lyrics that have "Verse 1 / Chorus" headings | FEAT-4 | Pasted lyrics come in already split into sections |
| 6 | Slide actions: a slide can start a background, music, a stage layout or a look; small icons on the thumbnail show what is attached | FEAT-2 | One click on the Intro slide shows the background and switches the stage layout |
| 7 | Looks: per screen, which layers show plus a theme override (centred lyrics in the room, lower-third lyrics on the livestream) | FEAT-1 / FEAT-7 | Switching a look changes only the screens it names |
| 8 | Stage layouts with live fields: current and next slide, next section, clock, countdown, slides left, stage message; 2–3 ready-made layouts | FEAT-3 | The stage screen updates as slides change, without the operator touching it |
| 9 | Announcement loop: drop a folder of images to get a timed, looping slideshow; walk-in music that does not restart when the loop starts over; an announcements lane that can go to one screen only (lobby or livestream) | FEAT-2 | The loop runs on the livestream while the room shows something else |
| 10 | Keyboard: type a number + Enter to jump to that slide; Up/Down for the next service item; hold a key for big-text view | FEAT-4 | Works with the existing C/B/P/E/1–9 keys |
| 11 | *New:* virtual output screen (e.g. 1920x1080 with no monitor) and NDI output so OBS can take it | FEAT-7 | OBS shows the livestream screen with no extra monitor plugged in |
| 12 | Coloured headers in the service list (Pre-service, Worship, Message, Post) | FEAT-6 | Headers can be added, renamed, coloured and dragged |
| 13 | Smart media folders: point at a folder on disk; the media library updates by itself | FEAT-1 (media library) | A file copied into the folder appears without restarting |
| 14 | Bundle export: one file with the service or item plus its media, so someone can build at home and send it in (builds on V1-BUNDLE) | FEAT-6 | Opening the file on another PC shows the same slides and media; missing fonts are named |
| 15 | Bible panel: choose which text box gets the verse and which gets the reference, verses per slide, "Add to service" | FEAT-5 | A passage can be added to the service without leaving the panel |

Still left out (from the video): SongSelect and Planning Center sign-in (paid; already FEAT-7 / §10 item 1), edge blending, SDI, Resi. Built-in streaming moved to §10d (after 1.0, user decision Nov 6).

### UI-MENU — classic menu bar (user request, November 6, 2026) — *new*

The user wants the usual menu bar along the top-left of the operator window, like most desktop programs: **File, Edit, View, Window, Settings, Help**. Clicking a word opens a drop-down menu; items can have sub-menus and show their keyboard shortcut.

- Build it with Slint's own `MenuBar` (Slint 1.18 is in `Cargo.toml`), so Windows shows a real native menu and keyboard users can open it with Alt.
- Every menu item calls an action that already exists (no logic only reachable from the menu). Buttons on screen stay; the menu is an extra, easier-to-find way in.
- Starting contents (to be confirmed in the spec):
  - **File:** New service, Open service…, Open recent ▸, Save, Save as…, Import ▸ (Songs from EasyWorship, Lyrics from clipboard, PowerPoint/PDF, Media), Export ▸ (Service bundle, Song), Print service order, Exit.
  - **Edit:** Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, Paste, Delete, Find… (Ctrl+F), Edit song, Edit arrangement.
  - **View:** Grid / Big-text / Outline view, Show media library, Show action icons, Zoom in / out.
  - **Window:** Room screen on/off, Stage screen on/off, Configure screens…, Looks…, Stage layouts…, Practice mode.
  - **Settings:** Preferences…, Songs, Bibles, Media folders, Themes, Keyboard shortcuts.
  - **Help:** Keyboard shortcuts, Check before service (preflight), Open logs folder, About RustyPresenter.
- Done when: every item works, shortcuts shown match the real ones, greyed out when it can't be used (e.g. Save with nothing changed), and a by-hand check on Windows.
- Order: first in the queue after the current merge, because it is small and every later feature gets a menu home.

## 10d. Livestreaming (after 1.0) — user decisions, November 6, 2026

- RustyPresenter gets its **own** "Go Live" streaming (built on the FFmpeg libraries), not OBS code. Licence is not a worry (the user would accept GPL), but OBS is C and plug-in shaped, so it is used only for ideas (reconnect logic, encoder settings).
- Destinations at the same time: **YouTube, Facebook Live, Instagram Live, X, TikTok, Twitch**, plus **Custom**: any RTMP/RTMPS server address + stream key (Vimeo, Restream, Boxcast, Dacast, a church's own server). Each destination: name, on/off, wide or tall.
- Instagram and TikTok only give stream keys to some accounts; tall (9:16) output needs its own layout.
- Needed: upload speed test with a warning, automatic reconnect, one button to start/stop all.
- Before this: §10c item 11 (virtual output / NDI) lets OBS take a clean feed.

- Differences from the plan:
  - `operator_monitor` takes the main monitor as a third argument, since a `MonitorId` does not say which is main.

- Branch `plan4-powerpoint` (`54e1039`, new ref), two commits on `plan4-integration` `6f78df6`:
  - `6166565` **fix(app): send live views in the order they were read.** Only `src/app.rs`. **Take this one even if nothing else comes across; it should go into every line of work.**
  - `54e1039` Task 6b: new `src/import/powerpoint.rs`, `src/import/powerpoint_tests.rs`; one line in `src/import/mod.rs`; three strings in `src/strings/import.rs`.
- **The bug, which affects a live service.** `broadcast_live` read the view and then sent it, with nothing holding the two together. The room's commit (on `room-events`) and an operator's Next (on a command thread) can both be in there. If the older view goes out second, it replaces the newer one in the room's `latest`, so the room never hears about the new slide. The engine stays pending and the screen does not change until the next update. In a service: Next pressed just as the previous slide commits seems to do nothing. The Tauri path shares this code.
  - `missing_still_asset_reports_failed_and_keeps_previous` and `logo_layer_follows_frame_logo` were failing for this reason, about 1 run in 12, on an idle machine. The 21:41 and 02:41 notes put it down to a busy machine; that was wrong. A trace of a failed run showed the room never saw `v2`.
  - Fix: `App.live_order`, a mutex held from reading the view to sending it. It is safe because `live_view` already takes `data` and `live`, so no caller of `broadcast_live` can hold either.
  - My first guess, a stale view reaching `RoomState::plan`, was checked and was wrong as stated: 45 runs printed nothing, because the two views coalesce in `latest` and `plan` only sees the old one.
- Task 6b, differences from the plan:
  - `export` is not `#[cfg(windows)]`, so all of its logic runs on Linux against a stand-in (the test binary run again, as in Task 5). Task 6c still only builds a `PowerPoint` when `powerpoint_installed()`.
  - `progress` is `&(dyn Fn(usize, usize) + Sync)`, not `&dyn Fn`: without `Sync` the future is not `Send` and Task 6c could not `tokio::spawn` it. `export_can_be_spawned` holds this.
  - `src/media.rs` is unchanged. `PPT_SCRIPT` and `export_powerpoint` stay until Task 6c deletes them, so the two live side by side and this branch does not touch a file the PC may be merging.
  - Added to the script: `$AutomationSecurity` is put back afterwards. It belongs to the whole PowerPoint, so leaving it at 3 would silently disable macros in the operator's own files for the rest of the session. `CLEANUP_SCRIPT` sets 1, what PowerPoint starts with.
  - Added: `$wasRunning` from `Get-Process`, so a PowerPoint the operator had open with nothing loaded does not count as started by us. It only reads; nothing kills by name.
  - `STARTED_BY_US` is printed before `Open`, so a hang inside `Open` still tells the cleanup the truth.
  - `$Path + '::x::'` instead of `"$Path::x::"`, so no question of how PowerShell parses the colons.
  - `[Console]::Out.WriteLine` and an invariant culture for `SIZE`, so lines arrive as they happen and a German Windows does not print `720,5`. Both scripts stay ASCII, as PowerShell 5.1 reads a file without a BOM in the old code page.
  - `export` checks the PNGs itself: every promised slide exists, at `output_size`, within 2 px. Any failure gives `PPT_CANNOT_OPEN`, so Task 6c falls back to LibreOffice. The rounding is done twice, in `f32` here and `double` there, hence the slack.
  - Messages: `PPT_TIMEOUT`, `PPT_CANNOT_OPEN`, `PPT_NOT_STARTED`. A refused password gives `sniff::ENCRYPTED`; this needs PowerPoint's English wording, so on another language it says `PPT_CANNOT_OPEN`. Raw errors go to the log.
  - `pptm_macro_does_not_run` is a manual check, as the plan allows. The two other gated tests are runtime gated (`RP_TEST_PPT=1`), not `cfg`, so they compile everywhere. PowerPoint makes their sample.
- Evidence (Linux sandbox, `rustc 1.97`, `plan4-integration` tree, without Tauri or libmpv, stubs for `console_error_panic_hook` and `console_log`):
  - `cargo test --offline --lib`: 649 passed, 0 failed, three runs in a row. `ui::room::tests` 70 runs, 0 failures; before the fix 1 run in about 12 failed.
  - `live_views_go_out_in_the_order_they_were_read` failed without the lock ("view 138 went out after view 148") and passes with it.
  - `import::powerpoint`: 30 tests, 2 of which return at once without `RP_TEST_PPT`. 20 failed against `todo!()`. Five mutations each failed the right tests: no cleanup, no gate, ignoring `STARTED_BY_US`, no size check, no longer limit after `COUNT`. The last passed at first, so `slide_count_extends_the_time_limit` was added and now catches it.

The stand-in is not in the repo.
  - clippy: nothing in the new files.

The Windows build was not tried: `rustup target add` crashed here. The module has no Windows-only code. Integration tests were not run on this branch, only `--lib`.

### November 3, 2026 — Claude (scheduled run, 12:44 UTC)

- Tasks: §11 service bundle, one of the open items from the 11:41 run's audit. Not merged. No PC branch or worktree was touched.
- **Branch `plan4-bundle` (`f67c6cd`, new ref, one commit on `plan4-checks` `938e2d0`).** It replaces `plan4-checks` as the one line of work.
- **Built:** Services dialog → **Save to file** and **Open from file**, for moving a service to another computer. `src/bundle.rs` (`gather`, `write`, `read`, `merge`), `App::export_service` and `App::import_service` (methods, not `/api/do` actions, so a phone cannot reach them), `files::pick_save`, `Filter::Services`. The file is a zip named `.rpservice`: `bundle.json` (deflated) and `assets/<id>` (stored). Both run as job cards with Cancel, and the dialog closes when the job starts so the card shows.
- **Decisions the spec does not settle. Jake can change these:**
  - A bundle carries the service and the songs, media and decks it uses. It does not carry settings, so the default background, logo and look stay as the other computer has them.
  - On opening, media and decks match by their files. A song matches by id, CCLI # or title, and only counts as the same with the same lyrics and arrangement. **A song with other words comes in as a second copy**, so the service shows what was saved and the library's song is untouched. The report names those songs. Without this, a song edited on the laptop would quietly show old words on the other computer.
  - The opened service becomes the open one. A name in use gets " (2)". All ids are new, so opening a file twice gives two services and no second copy of anything else.
  - Saving refuses if a song, media entry, deck or file is missing, and lists what. A missing thumbnail or original file is left out instead.
- An asset id becomes a path, so `read` checks every id in the file against `<64 hex>.<ext>`, needs each in the zip, and hashes each against its name. Files are staged, and published only when all have passed. `bundle.json` is read up to 64 MB.
- **Not built:** dropping a `.rpservice` on the window (it takes the media route and is refused). No rows for it in the phone remote, by design.

New tests `a_panic_in_the_headless_room_restarts_the_app`, `headless_logs_the_restored_frame_even_when_a_newer_one_waits`, `a_restore_that_never_reached_the_screen_says_so`. Not ported: the `Fresh` wrapper that removes test data folders, as this line's tests differ.
- **Built, §6.4:** new `src/sound.rs` (`missing`, `pauses`), `App::watch_sound`, `startup::watch_sound` every 3 s, spawned in `main`. If a sound output is chosen, something can be heard, and the chosen output is not in the list, the foreground video and the audio pause and the live column shows `SOUND_GONE`. Pressing play pauses again at the next look. The notice goes when the output is back or another is chosen; play stays the operator's. `Player::set_audio_device` and `Msg::Device` in the room move what is on air to a new choice, as before it applied only from the next item, so a paused video would have stayed on the dead output.
- **Decisions Jake can change:**
  - The background video is muted, so it plays on.
  - A foreground video with no sound track pauses too: nothing in `MediaFile` says it has none.
  - Each look starts a throwaway mpv handle. It runs only while a sound output is chosen and something can be heard, or the notice is up. With the default output nothing is watched, so unplugging the default output is not caught; that is Windows' choice of output, not a silent fallback from a chosen one.
- **Not guarded:** if WASAPI lists nothing but another driver lists something, a chosen `wasapi/` output reads as gone and media pauses. Only an empty list counts as "could not read". I think that is rare, but it has not been seen either way.
- **§8.4 manual Bible breaks is not a small job, so it stays open.** `ItemKind::Scripture` has no `VerseId`s yet; the dialog is FEAT-5 Task B10, which needs B1–B3, and Part B is marked blocked until Plans 3 and 4 are merged.

The cost of the first check on Windows (loading every system font, then fallback searches for rows with missing glyphs, up to 200 rows) was not measured.

  - **The ear, phoneme model (`ear.rs`).** Each heard sound keeps up to three sounds the model nearly wrote instead (`Sound::alts`: at least a quarter of the first guess's probability, and 0.05); taking one of them for the word's sound costs 0.25 to 0.75 of an edit, not 1. A word of two sounds ("the", "to", "you") may differ by one close second guess. With no second guesses everything is as it was (all the old ear tests pass unchanged).
  - **The ear, letter model (`ear.rs`).** A written word that is no word of the song is held against the song's words (`Lexicon::spelled`): a letter or two off, it is that song word (`whole`: up to 3 letters only by a close second guess, 4–5 letters one edit, 6 and more two); it may be the second half of the word before ("HALLE|LUJAH"); or several song words written as one ("AMAZINGRACE", `run_together`). Otherwise it waits one word (it may be a half) and is heard as written. A word that reaches 64 letters with no break is decided and keeps its unfinished end. Without a song: as before.
  - **Pronunciations (`g2p.rs`).** A printout of about 550 common worship words showed many that the rules built two sounds or more from how they are said (or one, in a word too short to spare one), so the phoneme ear never heard them: "water" was "weɪtɚ", "two" "twoʊ", "us" "ʌz", "gone" "ɡoʊn", "heard" "hɪɹd", "body" "boʊdi", "promise" "pɹoʊmaɪz", "together" "toʊdʒɛðɚ", "away" "ɔːeɪ". 167 such words are in `EXCEPTIONS` now (their plurals, "-ed", "-ing", "-ful" come through it); "-ore" is "oːɹ"; a stem ending in "c" keeps its "e" ("dancing", "rejoiced"); "-fy" ends in "aɪ". The IPA is from memory of espeak en-us, not checked against espeak.

Seen: with a dead destination the stream starts about 2 s late (Windows takes that long to refuse a closed port), and those seconds are not in the recording either. `quick-check record`: 58 passed. **Not verified:** YouTube, Facebook or Twitch themselves; RTMPS; SRT; a sound input in a stream; the button and Settings rows on screen. **Not done:** the 27 livestream texts in the 12 languages other than Spanish.
  - **Reconnect (Jake: "Yes, reconnect").** `src/stream/send.rs`: the recorder's `tee` writes a local UDP MPEG-TS feed per destination (`free_ports`, `feed`; `bsfs/v=dump_extra` repeats the picture's header at every keyframe); `keep_sending` runs one FFmpeg per destination that copies the feed out (`sender_args`: `-analyzeduration 3s` so a sender joining mid-stream sees a keyframe; FLV with `aac_adtstoasc`, or MPEG-TS for SRT) and restarts it after 2/4/8/16/30 s (from 2 again after 30 s up). `Recording::links` → `Operator::say_links` toasts "going out" / "lost its connection, trying again" / back. **Found by trying it:** the stand-in `anullsrc` ran ~10 s ahead of the picture and a late-joining sender got nothing ("non-existing PPS"); `-shortest_buf_duration 0.5` holds it back (`-re` instead stalled FFmpeg 11 s at the start). **Real test** `go_live_reaches_a_server_survives_a_dead_one_and_comes_back_after_a_drop`: the RTMP server is killed at 8 s and a new one listens; the stream returns to it by itself with H.264 + AAC, the recording is whole (about 30 s to run). A reconnect takes about 7 s (wait, next keyframe, 3 s looking at the feed). quick-check `record` 59, `stream` 17, `translation_editor` 4. **Not verified:** a real service; a real internet drop (only a server going away); with a sound input. Texts: template and Spanish only.
  - **Livestream texts in all 13 languages** (machine-drafted, each catalog's own words for Settings / Media / Record). Spanish was redone: it had "usted" and "Ajustes → Medios" where the catalog says "tú" and "Configuración → Multimedia". `translations/allow-bare.txt` gained YouTube, Facebook, Twitch. **Missed earlier:** `tests/i18n_catalogs` (a separate test program, not in a filtered `quick-check`) failed from `bcaee9f` to here on those three bare names. When a change touches `.slint` text or `translations/`, also run `cargo test --profile quick --test i18n_catalogs`.
  - **Disk cleanup (Jake: "Storage is almost full", Nov 14).** C: had 16.9 GB free. Removed rebuildable output only: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\RustyPresenter\build` (24 GB, the Tauri-era scripts' folder), `build-check\quick\incremental` (26.5 GB), `build-check\release\examples` (7.6 GB), `build-check\review`, `experiments`, and 14,287 `rp-*` test scraps in `%TEMP%`: 68 GB free after. The tests leave those scraps behind (`rp-test-*` from `test_app`, `rp-panic*`, `rp-preflight*`): worth fixing.
  - **The PC's sound in a livestream (Jake: "Everything the PC plays", then "nah we get to decide devices").** Settings → Media → Livestream → "Sound from this computer": Off / the output the app plays to (default, `RecordPrefs::stream_sound` empty) / any output from libmpv's list (`wasapi/{id}`). `src/stream/pc_sound.rs`: WASAPI loopback of that endpoint (`windows` 0.62, features Win32_Media_Audio / System_Com / Com_StructuredStorage / System_Variant; no new download), silence filled by the clock (`silence_needed`), sent to the recorder's FFmpeg on `tcp://127.0.0.1:P?listen=1` as f32le/s16le; `args::Record.pc` maps it alone or `amix` with the sound input. **Found by trying it:** `-shortest` without `-shortest_buf_duration 0.5` made FFmpeg stop reading the PC's sound after 0.7 s (reproduced by hand with a Python feeder). **Real tests:** the default output is captured at the clock's pace; ignored `go_live_sends_what_the_pc_plays` (plays a quiet tone through FFplay) → the stream and the recording have it at −34.6 dB, silence before. This PC has VoiceMeeter, Nahimic and virtual cables; only the default output was tried. Recording without Go Live is unchanged (the app's sound is still mixed from the files afterwards). macOS/Linux: no capture yet.
  - **Test leftovers (Jake: "Yes, fix the tests").** `app::sweep_test_scraps`, run once per test process from `test_app`: Temp entries whose names start with one of `TEST_SCRAPS` (34 prefixes, each checked to be made only by tests) and that are over two hours old are removed. `rp-import-` and `rp-scripture-` are the app's own and are never swept. At most about two hours of runs' leftovers stay. Test `old_test_leftovers_are_swept_and_nothing_else`.
  - **Live button health (Jake: "cooook").** `LiveBtn.up/total` from `say_links`: fewer than all connected → amber "Live 1/2" (screen readers: "1 of 2 connected"); all → red "Live". Right after Go Live it shows amber 0/N for the ~4 s each sender needs to count as up. Not seen on screen.
  - **CMU Pronouncing Dictionary (Jake: "Yes, download it").** `assets/cmudict/cmudict-ipa.tsv` (2.6 MB, 121,735 words / 130,613 ways, from cmusphinx/cmudict master on Nov 14, ARPAbet → IPA as the README says) is `include_str!`'d in `g2p.rs`; `g2p::variants` = the hand table's way if any, else every dictionary way within one sound of the first (spelled-out letters like "am" = A.M. dropped after they broke `i_is_not_heard_as_im`), else the rules. `phonemes()` is unchanged (rules), so it no longer says what the ear listens for. Licence: BSD-style, in `assets/cmudict/LICENSE`, `packaging/licenses/cmudict-LICENSE.txt`, `native.txt` and `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` (hand-added in the script's format; `scripts/licenses.sh` lists it). Not measured on real singing.
  - **Licence reminders (Jake: "Yes, add reminders", after asking whether the project had done anything wrong).** First Go Live on a computer: a one-time note (`Operator::note_stream_licence`, `RecordPrefs::licence_noted`) that streaming worship songs needs a streaming licence (e.g. CCLI Streaming) as well as the usual one, and that videos or music in the stream need their owners' permission. Download from YouTube already said "Only download videos your church has the right to use (your own, licensed, or Creative Commons)": left as it is.
  - **Camera in the livestream (Jake: "COOK HARDER MAN"; spec 1.3b, picture-in-picture only).** Settings → Livestream → Camera (`RecordPrefs::camera`, `camera_main`; list from `run::list_cameras` / `args::parse_cameras`, Windows dshow and macOS avfoundation, Linux none). `args::Camera` + `picture_in_picture`: one input fills 1280×720/1920×1080 (shape kept, black bars), the other a quarter as wide bottom right; `-map [v]`. Camera-filling ends with the room screen (`eof_action=endall`: without it Stop never ended FFmpeg and the file was lost); screen-filling keeps a stopped camera's last picture. Real test with a lavfi stand-in camera; this PC has an "Integrated Camera" that was **not** switched on (it would film whoever is there). **Not built:** lyrics as a lower third over the camera (needs the room picture without its background), several cameras, switching, camera in a plain recording.
  - **Words over the camera (lower third).** `args::Layout::CameraWords` (Settings: "Camera, with the words over it"; `RecordPrefs::camera_layout` 0/1/2 replaced `camera_main`). `stream::lyrics`: `key_for` makes a `RenderKey` of the live `SlideLayer::Text` in `lower_third(theme)` style (position lower, band, size 64/52, the slide's font and colour), `Feed` draws it with its own `Fonts` at 10 fps (clock-paced) as premultiplied RGBA to `tcp://…?listen=1`; `camera_with_words` overlays it (`alpha=premultiplied`) and keeps the room screen as an invisible 2×2 dot so Stop still ends the picture. Real test frame looked at: the words on their band at the bottom of the stand-in camera. **Not done:** words in a plain recording (only while live), the slide's own size, designed slides.
  - **Too-slow internet warning (instead of a speed test, which would need a test server).** `send::Link { up, skips }`; a sender's "Circular buffer overrun" lines (`send::skipped`) count as skips; at 3 (`SLOW_SKIPS`) `say_links` tells the operator once per destination per stream (`tr::too_slow`). Not seen on a real slow line. Also: a fresh release build of `5d5d29c` and a new `RustyPresenter-portable.zip` on the Desktop (81 MB, 22:52 Nov 14).
  - **Real webcam, once (Jake: "Yes, use the webcam").** Ignored test `real_camera_goes_live_with_the_words` (`RP_REAL_CAMERA="Integrated Camera"`): 10 s live to a local RTMP server with the words over the camera; 9.8 s of 1280×720 arrived, the picture varied (brightness spread 199), no problem; the clip and frame were deleted unseen. FFmpeg noted "No accelerated colorspace conversion from yuva420p to argb" (speed, not a fault). Still not tried: a capture card, a camera unplugged while live, a real service.
  - **A camera that stops no longer stops the stream (Jake: "COOK. Whats next?").** Found by test: with the camera as the overlay's base, a camera ending ended FFmpeg and with it the stream and the recording. `picture_in_picture` / `camera_with_words` now always use the room screen as base and clock, camera over it with `eof_action=pass` (screen shows instead), words over that. Test `a_camera_that_stops_does_not_stop_the_stream` (stand-in camera ending at 2 s, all three layouts). Not re-run with the real webcam (Jake agreed to one 10-second test). Not handled: a camera that comes back (it stays out until the next Go Live).
  - **Camera rejoins (Jake: "Yes, camera rejoins").** `stream::camera_feed`: the camera's own FFmpeg (`camera_args`: scaled, yuva420p, rawvideo on stdout) on its own thread; the feed passes the newest picture (≤ 0.5 s old) at 30 fps to `tcp://…?listen=1` (`input_args`), else a see-through one (`clear_frame`), restarting the camera 2 s (`RETRY`) after it stops. `args::Camera.feed_port`; the layouts keep the camera's alpha. `Recording::camera_seen` → `Operator::say_camera` toasts lost/back. Test `a_camera_that_stops_drops_out_and_comes_back`. Cost: about 2.3 MB per picture at 1280×720 over loopback (69 MB/s); not measured on a slow PC. Not tried: the real webcam unplugged and plugged back.
  - **Stage notes never cut off (Jake, Nov 15: "Sometimes notes cut off when there are too much").** `ui::stage::fit_between` goes on below a box's `min_size` in 0.2 % steps to `FLOOR` (1.2 %) before giving up (it returned `min_size` and the rest was clipped). Classic's notes box: `max-height: 30vh`, `notes-size` set by `fit_notes` (3.2 % down). Tests `long_notes_*` (ten paragraphs: Slides and notes 2.4 %, Speaker 1.8 %). A whole page in a tiny box can still overflow at the floor. Not seen on a real stage screen. The phone's stage view (`web/stage-view.js` `fitAll`) does the same: below the box's smallest size in 0.2 % steps to 1.2 %; checked in the browser pane with ten paragraphs of notes in a 3–6 % box (overflowed at 3 %, fits at 2 %). Not seen on a real phone.
  - **No-sound warning (Jake: "KEEP THE WORK GOING"; spec §6 "Silence > 10 s: warning").** With a sound input (recording or live) FFmpeg runs `silencedetect=noise=-60dB:duration=10` on that input only (`args::listen`, `SILENCE`; with the PC's sound it sits before the `amix`, as the PC is often quiet). Its note is an info line, so the recorder's FFmpeg logs at `level+info -nostats` then, and `run::log_line` keeps only that note besides warnings and errors (their `[warning]` tag taken off; without a sound input it stays `warning` as before). `Recording::silent` → `Operator::say_silence` toasts "No sound has come from {name} for 10 seconds. Check the cable, and that the sound board is sending to this computer." once, and "Sound from {name} is coming in again." 2 texts in 13 languages (machine-drafted). Real FFmpeg: the note comes after 10 s of silence and not 1 s sooner; the camera layout's `-filter_complex` and the `-af` work together. quick-check `record` 70 passed (one unseen failure in an earlier run, then two clean runs), `translation_editor` 4, `i18n_catalogs` 4. **Not verified:** a real sound board or a muted channel's actual level (−60 dB is reasoned); a lead-vocal-only send is silent through the sermon and will warn then.
  - **Panic buttons: Freeze and Mute (Jake: "Yes, panic buttons"; spec §6).** Beside Live while live (`PanicBtn` in `record.slint`; amber with "Frozen" / "Muted" when on; off again at Stop). Named **Freeze**, not Hold: auto-follow's Hold is in the same top bar ("Pause" in its translations). `run::Controls { hold, mute }` in `Plan`, `Recording::hold/mute`, `Operator::hold_stream/mute_stream`. The file and the stream are one encode, so the recording freezes and goes quiet too.
    - **Freeze:** the pump hands new room pictures back unused; `camera_feed` keeps sending its last picture; `lyrics` keeps its words. Test `hold_keeps_the_picture_until_it_is_let_go` (real FFmpeg: red kept while the room is green, green after).
    - **Mute:** the app's own FFmpeg (the one churches download) cannot take commands while it runs (no `zmq`), so the sound input now passes through the app: `stream::board_feed` runs a small FFmpeg on the input (s16le 48 kHz stereo), passes the sound on over local TCP, zeroes it while muted, fills silence by the clock while the input is gone and opens it again every 2 s. `pc_sound` zeroes while muted. This replaces the FFmpeg `silencedetect` of the commit before: the level is watched in `board_feed::Quiet` (peak ≤ 33, about −60 dB, for 10 s). **Behaviour change:** a sound input that cannot open, or is unplugged, no longer ends the recording; it goes on silent and the no-sound warning names the input (test renamed `an_input_that_cannot_open_gives_silence_and_the_recording_goes_on`). If `board_feed` cannot start, FFmpeg reads the input itself as before. Tests with a real FFmpeg as the input: sound at the clock's pace and quiet noticed 10 s after it began; muted mid-tone gives silence and unmuted the tone again.
    - **Not verified:** a real sound board through `board_feed` (latency added by the extra hop is not measured; a/v sync on a long service); the buttons on screen; a real stream service.
  - **Not done:** Frame-level alignment (one pass over the song's sounds with the model's probabilities, no words in between) is the long-term design; what the listening log shows on a real Sunday should decide whether it is needed.

### November 15, 2026 — Claude (livestream scenes, 1.3b, branch `follow-sunday`)

- Merged `graphics-test-v2` (the graphics test measures the real app). Then livestream scenes, design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-15-livestream-scenes-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-15-livestream-scenes.md`.
- **The app composes the stream picture itself** (`stream::compose`, `record::run::Mixer`): room, camera (RGBA kept in memory by `stream::camera_feed`), words and cards (`stream::pictures`), half-second fades. FFmpeg gets one plain input; the old filter graphs and `camera_layout` are gone.
- **Scenes:** Camera, Camera + words, Full screen, Screen + camera, Starting soon (with the pre-service countdown), Ending, Hold. Chosen by `stream::scene::choose` from `App::stream_info` ten times a second; no camera → room screen. Scene button beside Live (Automatic + each scene; a pick holds until the next item); slide action / macro step "Livestream: …" (`MacroStep::StreamScene`); Settings → Media → Livestream: Automatic scenes, Presentations, the three cards (words or a picture).
- Tests: stream:: record:: automation:: ui::operator::record 263 passed, ui::dialogs i18n 373 passed, i18n_catalogs 4; real FFmpeg on Windows: camera with bars, words over camera, scene switch mid-stream, camera unplugged and back.
- Translations: 36 texts drafted in all 13 languages (incl. 15 left by the graphics test), not reviewed.
- **Not verified:** a real camera and a real stream with scenes; the scene button and settings by eye.
- **Later the same night (Jake: "Just keep adding features and checking for bugs", then "JUST FINISH THE PROJECT"):** sound meter beside Mute (`stream::level`); sound delay (Settings → Livestream, `adelay` on the stream's sound, at most 1 s); upload speed test (Settings → Livestream, 4 MB of zeros to speed.cloudflare.com, picks 720p/1080p for the destinations on); a second camera (Settings → Livestream → Second camera; switched from the scene button's list while live, `Controls::camera`). Fixed: Ending with headers/blanks after the last item; cards were put together again for every camera picture; Starting soon after a restart. All with tests (real FFmpeg for the camera switch and the delay). Flaky under heavy load once: `a_sound_delay_keeps_the_stream_going` (file 0.3 s, board feed reset) and `go_live_shows_the_camera_with_black_bars` (local RTMP server did not start); both pass alone and in a full `record::` run. Not looked into further.
- **Still to do for the livestream (design §8):** sound mixer with per-source levels and filters (1.3c), NDI/RTSP/phone cameras and PTZ (1.3d), YouTube/Facebook/Twitch sign-in (1.3e, needs the church's developer accounts), scene editor, tall canvas, NDI out (1.3f). Then Mac/Linux.

### November 16, 2026 — Claude (livestream sign-in, 1.3e, branch `follow-sunday`)

- **Jake: sign-in uses the church's own Google and Meta developer accounts.** Setup steps for him: `docs/livestream-sign-in-setup.md`. The four ids are build-time (`option_env!` `RP_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `RP_GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`, `RP_FACEBOOK_APP_ID`, `RP_FACEBOOK_CLIENT_TOKEN`), from a git-ignored `.tools\sign-in-ids.cmd` that `try-latest.cmd`, `windows-check.cmd` and `make-installer.cmd` call. Without them no Sign in button shows; stream keys work as before.
- `stream::accounts`: YouTube = Google's desktop sign-in (browser, loopback `127.0.0.1`, PKCE), then the Live Streaming API: one reusable stream named "RustyPresenter" (made once), a broadcast per Go Live with title/description/privacy, auto start and stop, bound to it; viewers from `videos.liveStreamingDetails`. Facebook = Login for Devices (code typed at facebook.com/device), the first Page the account manages, its token (does not run out); `live_videos` with `LIVE_NOW`, ended at Stop; `live_views`.
- What sign-in keeps (refresh token / Page id and token) is in the password store under `accounts::token_id(destination)`, with the account's name. Settings → Media → Livestream: Sign in / Signed in as … / Sign out per row; Broadcast title (`{date}`, `{service}`; default "Sunday Service — {date}", translated), description, YouTube's who can watch. Go Live: signed-in rows make their broadcast off the UI thread (a failure is said for that row only); the Live button shows "Live · N watching" (asked every 30 s); Stop says each replay link.
- Tests against a stand-in Google/Facebook on this computer (`accounts::testing::serve`): sign-in both ways, refusal, revoked sign-in, broadcast made and reused stream, Facebook ended; UI: Facebook sign-in/out in Settings, title saved, Go Live signed in with real FFmpeg. quick-check `record stream translation settings` 305 passed.
- Full quick-check: 3234 passed, 1 failed (`a_camera_that_stops_drops_out_and_comes_back`, real FFmpeg under the full suite's load); a `record::run` run alone then failed `go_live_shows_the_camera_with_black_bars` (blue at the side at 3 s) and `go_live_puts_the_words_over_the_camera` (the sender's UDP feed: "bind failed", address in use); `record::run::tests::go_live` alone passed twice. The same flakiness as November 15; not caused by sign-in (no change under `record::run`). Worth a look: `send::free_ports` releases its ports before FFmpeg binds them.
- **Not verified:** the real Google and Facebook (no ids yet); whether Facebook's device login grants the Page permissions in Development mode (the docs say only "permissions approved for Login"); Twitch sign-in not built; chat, thumbnails and scheduling not built.

### November 16, 2026 (later) — Claude (network cameras, flaky stream tests; branch `follow-sunday`)

- **Flaky stream tests, two causes.** (1) Port race: `send::free_ports`, `board_feed` and `pc_sound` took ports the system hands out (bind port 0) and freed them for FFmpeg, and FFmpeg's own sending sockets could take them first ("bind failed"). New `platform::ports` (`udp(n)`, `tcp()`): ports from 20000–31999, below every system's automatic range, handed out one after another within the app (start picked by process id). Tests `feed_ports_are_ones_the_system_does_not_hand_out_and_never_repeat` (failed first: 59313), `a_port_in_use_is_passed_over`. (2) Under the full suite's load the stand-in camera's FFmpeg can start after 3 s, so `go_live_shows_the_camera_with_black_bars` saw black at 3 s; the stream showing black until the camera's first picture is right. Both camera-in-stream tests now check the first picture (2–7 s) that has the camera, and the bars/words on that picture.
- **Network cameras (part of 1.3d).** Each Camera list in Settings → Media → Livestream ends with "Network camera"; choosing it shows an address field (rtsp, rtsps, srt, rtmp(s), http(s), udp, rtp, tcp). The address is saved as the camera; `record::args::camera_input` opens it (RTSP over TCP, 5 s timeout; the camera feed tries again as for a USB camera). The scene button names it "Network camera <host>", never with a password from the address. Tests: `a_network_camera_is_opened_by_its_address`, `settings_keep_a_network_camera_by_its_address`, `a_network_camera_sends_its_pictures` (real FFmpeg serving red pictures over HTTP). Texts drafted in all 13 languages, not reviewed.
- Full quick-check: 3240 passed, 0 failed; integration tests all pass.
- **Not verified:** a real IP camera or NDI|HX/OBS over SRT; the address (with any password) is kept in the settings file, not the password store. Not built: NDI, phone cameras, PTZ.
- **Stream sound (part of 1.3c).** `record::args::SoundFx` (delay, `board_db`, `pc_db`, `even`, `quiet_noise`) replaces the delay alone; `RecordPrefs::sound_fx()` from new prefs `stream_board_db`, `stream_pc_db` (within `MAX_LEVEL_DB` = 20), `stream_even` (`None` = on), `stream_quiet_noise`. Filters: microphone/line-in `afftdn=nf=-25` then `volume`; PC sound `volume`; on all of it `adelay` then `EVEN_OUT` (acompressor −18 dB 3:1 +6 dB, alimiter −1 dB). Live only, like the delay. Settings → Media → Livestream: "Sound levels" (two sliders, −20…+20 dB) and "Sound clean-up" (Even out: on by default; hum and hiss: off, it can dull music). The meter beside Mute still shows the sound before these. Tests: `the_sound_can_be_turned_up_cleaned_and_evened_out`, `the_streams_sound_settings_are_kept_and_given_to_the_recorder`, `settings_keep_the_streams_sound_levels_and_clean_up`, `ffmpeg_takes_the_sound_filters` (real FFmpeg). Not listened to by a person.
- **One more timing test made sturdy:** `a_camera_that_stops_drops_out_and_comes_back` now reads a picture every quarter second and checks the order camera → room screen → camera, not fixed times. Full quick-check: 3244 passed, 0 failed.
- **"PC too slow" (design §6).** `record::pump::Due::lag`; the writer keeps the most it was behind since asked (`Recording::lag`); `record::slow::Slow` (behind > 0.5 s in 10 of the last 20 seconds → `TooSlow`; a whole 20 s kept up → `Smooth`); the operator's 1 s ticker toasts `record::tr::too_slow` (720p under Picture size, or close programs) and `smooth_again`. For recording and live. Tests: `a_hiccup_is_nothing_but_falling_behind_again_and_again_is_said_once` (failed first), `exactly_the_limit_is_not_behind`, `how_far_behind_the_writer_is`, `a_recording_says_how_far_behind_it_is` (real FFmpeg), `the_operator_hears_when_the_computer_cannot_keep_up_and_when_it_does_again`. Not seen on a slow computer.
- **Crash while live (design §6).** `record::unfinished`: `recording-under-way.json` in the data folder while a recording runs (`file`, `live`); cleared when it ends and when the app closes normally (`main.rs` after the event loop). At start `Operator::after_unfinished_recording` (called in `main.rs` after the startup notices): finishes the part into a video (`run::finish_leftovers`) and toasts where; after a livestream asks "RustyPresenter stopped in the middle of a livestream… Go live again?" (Go Live / Not now). Tests: `a_recording_that_never_ended_is_found_once`, `after_the_app_stopped_while_live_it_asks_to_go_live_again`, note checks in `record_then_stop_saves_an_mp4_in_the_chosen_folder` (all failed first). `cargo check --bin` passes; the startup path itself is not run by a test. Full quick-check 3251 passed, 0 failed.
- **Chapters in recordings (design §4.7).** `record::chapters`: `Source` (the live item's title, from `live.item.title`, set by `Operator::start_recording` for every recording), `Log::note` (a chapter when another item goes live; nothing live keeps the last chapter), `metadata` (FFmpeg's `;FFMETADATA1`, ms, `= ; # \` escaped, last chapter to the end). The writer looks once a second; `Notes` keep `chapters` and `secs` so a crashed recording keeps its chapters; `finish_file` writes `<name>.chapters.txt`, `args::finish` reads it (`-map_metadata`/`-map_chapters` = the input after the sound files), and finishes without chapters if that fails. Tests: `a_chapter_starts_at_each_new_item_taken_live`, `ffmpeg_reads_the_chapters_with_their_names_kept_whole`, `finishing_puts_the_chapters_in`, `a_recording_has_a_chapter_at_each_item` (real FFmpeg + ffprobe: "Welcome", "Amazing Grace"), all failed first. Not tested: the operator's `Source` closure itself. `a_camera_that_stops_drops_out_and_comes_back` also counts the green room (after half way) as the room. Full quick-check 3255 passed.
- **Two silent failures on Go Live fixed.** (1) A camera that never sends a picture (unplugged, or another program has it) said nothing: `say_camera` only spoke after a first picture. Now after `CAMERA_START_SECS` (15) without one: `stream::tr::camera_never`; "back" when it comes. (2) A destination that never connects (wrong key, refused, no internet) said nothing: `say_links` only spoke on up/down changes. `send::Link::failed` counts tries in a row that ended without connecting; at `NEVER_TRIES` (3) the operator is told once (`stream::tr::never_connected`, check the key and the internet). Tests: `a_camera_that_never_starts_is_said_and_said_again_when_it_comes`, `a_destination_that_never_connects_is_said_once`, `a_sender_that_cannot_connect_counts_its_tries` (all failed first). Full quick-check 3258 passed.
- **PTZ camera positions (1.3d).** `stream::ptz`: VISCA over IP recall-preset (`8x 01 04 3F 02 pp FF`), Sony's 8-byte header on port 52381 (the default), the bare command on any other port (PTZOptics: `:1259`); one UDP packet, not waited for. Settings → Media → Livestream: "Camera control address" under each camera (`RecordPrefs::camera_control`, `camera2_control`). While live the scene button lists "Camera position 1–6" for the camera in the picture (`Operator::show_positions`, `pick_position`). Macro / slide action step `MacroStep::CameraPosition { camera, position }` ("Camera 2: position 3", Stage group; macro editor "Move a PTZ camera"), run by `App::move_camera` whether live or not. The positions are saved with the camera's own remote. Calendar entries now pick their saved scene / camera position again when edited. Tests: `a_camera_control_address_is_read_with_its_port`, `a_position_is_called_back_with_visca`, `the_camera_gets_the_packet`, `settings_keep_each_cameras_control_address`, `camera_positions_go_to_the_camera_in_the_picture`, `camera_position_step_round_trips`, `a_slide_action_can_move_a_ptz_camera`, `a_camera_that_can_be_moved_has_its_positions_as_actions`, `a_macro_step_can_move_a_ptz_camera` (fake cameras on UDP). **Not verified:** a real PTZ camera; the menu by eye. Full quick-check 3267 passed.
- **Clips (design §4.7, "sermon clips").** `record::clips`: `Marks` (Clip pressed: start; again: end; a slip under 1 s left out; one open at the end runs to the end), `name` ("<recording> clip N.mp4", never over a file), `args`/`cut` (copied with `-ss … -t … -c copy`, no new encode). The Clip button beside Record while recording (`ClipBtn`, `Operator::toggle_clip`); slide action / macro step `MacroStep::Clip { start }` ("Start a clip of the recording", "End the clip") through `App::clip_request`, taken by the operator's 1 s ticker. Cut after the file is finished (`recording_ended`), each toasted. Tests: four in `record::clips` (one cuts a real video), `a_clip_marked_while_recording_is_saved_as_its_own_video` (real FFmpeg), `clip_steps_round_trip`, `a_slide_action_can_start_and_end_a_clip`.
- **Tall stream for phones (1.3f "wide + tall").** `Destination::tall`; Settings → Livestream "Tall for phones" (a box per destination). The recorder's own FFmpeg makes it: a second `-filter_complex` scales the wide picture across a 9:16 frame (`args::tall_size`: 720×1280 for 720p), black above and below, encoded as a second output to the tall destinations only (`stream::tee_only`); the sound is shared (`asplit` when mixed, the same `-af` otherwise). The file stays wide. Tests: `a_tall_stream_goes_out_beside_the_wide_one`, `go_live_sends_a_tall_stream_to_a_tall_destination` (real: the server gets 720×1280 with the picture across the middle, the file 1280×720), `settings_keep_which_destinations_get_the_tall_stream`.
- **Summary after a stream.** `stream::tr::summary`: how long it ran, and per destination "connected the whole time" / "dropped N times and came back" / "never connected" (`RecordUi::history`, from `say_links`). Test: `stopping_the_stream_says_how_it_went` (written with the code, not seen failing first).

The mixer places the corner camera by it (`corner_rect(size, layout)`); the words are drawn at the top with `lyrics::key_for_at`. Tests: `the_corner_camera_goes_where_the_church_put_it`, `screen_and_camera_puts_the_camera_in_the_chosen_corner`, the words-at-the-top check in `the_words_take_the_lower_third_look_in_the_slides_own_font`, `settings_keep_the_scene_layout` (written with the code). A chosen encoder is used only when this FFmpeg has it and `run::encoder_works` encodes three pictures with it; otherwise Automatic. Tests: `a_chosen_encoder_is_used_only_when_it_works_here`, `a_bitrate_set_by_hand_replaces_the_presets`, `an_encoder_is_tried_before_it_is_used` (real), `settings_keep_the_encoder_and_bitrate`.

Its refresh token works once: `Live::renewed` is kept again after Go Live. Settings' Twitch row has Sign in / Signed in as / Sign out. Setup steps added to `docs/livestream-sign-in-setup.md`. Twitch's renewed refresh token (each works once) is kept in the password store at once. Tests: `a_youtube_broadcast_keeps_its_sign_in_fresh_through_a_long_service`, `a_twitch_renewal_while_live_is_kept`.

One `Board` (newest 200, total count). The Chat button beside Live (signed-in broadcasts only) shows how many came since it was last opened; the list shows "Name · Service: text". New permissions: Facebook `pages_read_user_content`, Twitch `chat:read` (sign in again after this build). Tests: `chat_is_read_from_youtube_and_facebook_once_each`, `twitch_chat_is_read_as_it_is_written` (a stand-in IRC server), `a_twitch_chat_line_is_read_with_the_writers_shown_name`, `the_board_keeps_the_newest`, `the_chat_button_counts_new_messages_until_it_is_opened`.

Tests: `a_youtube_broadcast_gets_the_churchs_thumbnail`, `settings_keep_the_broadcast_thumbnail`.

Go Live binds the stream to it if it is still to come, else makes a new one; once used it is forgotten. Kept in the Settings form (`stream-scheduled-id`/`-link`) so Save keeps it. Tests: `a_broadcast_made_ahead_is_the_one_go_live_uses`, `a_broadcast_made_ahead_is_for_the_services_day_and_time`, `settings_make_next_sundays_youtube_broadcast_ahead`.

### November 16, 2026 (evening) — Claude (stream delay, lighter stream, tally, sound tracks; branch `follow-sunday`)

- **Built (with tests, texts drafted in 13 languages, not reviewed):**
  - **Stream delay** (Settings → Media → Livestream: None, 10 s, 30 s, 1 minute; `stream::delay`). A relay in the app holds each destination's local feed back before its sender, so Hold or Mute can stop something before viewers see it. The recording is not delayed. At Stop, what is still held goes out first.
  - **Lighter stream where the internet is slow** (on by default; `stream::send::Ladder`). A destination that keeps skipping gets its own sender that encodes again at 70%, 50%, then 35% of the bitrate (never under 800 kbit/s), and goes back up one step after five minutes without skips. Only that destination changes; the recording and the others keep the full picture. The operator is told when it changes. "Too slow" is said only once it is as light as it goes. Each change restarts that one sender (a few seconds' gap there).
  - **Phone tally lights**: `http://<presentation PC>:<port>/stage-view/tally?camera=1` (or 2) on a phone fixed to the camera. Red while that camera is in the stream, green while it is the camera waiting for the next camera scene, dark otherwise. Same access and password as the stage view (`stream::tally`, `network::stage_view`).
  - **Sound tracks in the recording** (off by default): when streaming with both a mic/line-in and the computer's sound, the file also keeps each on its own track after the mix; destinations still get only the mix (tee `select`). Tried by hand with FFmpeg 9: MKV and MP4 keep three tracks, FLV/MPEG-TS get one.
- **Not verified by a person:** all of the above against real services (delay and lighter stream with a real YouTube/Twitch, CPU cost of the lighter stream on an old PC), the tally page on a real phone, the new Settings rows by eye.
- Full quick-check at the end of this batch: 3309 app tests passed, 0 failed; every test in `tests/` passes (the web-folder list in `tests/cleanup.rs` now includes `tally.html`).
- **Disk:** the C: drive ran out of space during a build (`build-check` is about 58 GB). The quick build's `incremental` cache was deleted (it rebuilds itself). Worth clearing old build folders now and then.
- **Still to do (design §8):** see the next handoff (NDI is done there).

### November 16, 2026 (night) — Claude (NDI in and out; branch `follow-sunday`)

- **NDI runtime:** Jake approved installing it, and NDI Tools 6.3.2 (winget `NDI.NDITools`) is installed on the development PC. The app never ships or links NDI: `stream::ndi` loads `Processing.NDI.Lib.x64.dll` at run time from `NDI_RUNTIME_DIR_V6` (or V5), as libmpv is loaded. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-16-ndi-in-and-out-design.md`.
- **Built (with tests; texts drafted in 13 languages, not reviewed):**
  - **NDI cameras:** Settings → Media → Livestream → Camera lists the NDI sources on the network ("NDI: PC (OBS)"), saved as `ndi://<name>`. The camera feed receives them over NDI instead of FFmpeg, so the lost-camera light, switching and tally work as for any camera.
  - **NDI out** (two new checks under "NDI", off by default; they work while live): "RustyPresenter Stream" carries the stream's picture (before encoding) and its sound mix. The recorder's FFmpeg sends a raw copy of the mix to the app. "RustyPresenter Words" carries the words over the camera on a see-through background, for keying in OBS/vMix/ATEM.
  - Without NDI Tools: Settings says to install them, and Go Live with NDI on says nothing goes out over NDI.
- **Checked on this PC:** a picture sent as an NDI source is found on the network and comes back through the NDI camera receiver (test `stream::ndi::tests::a_picture_sent_over_ndi_comes_back`, which is skipped where NDI is not installed). FFmpeg's raw sound output to the app was checked by hand.
- **Not verified by a person:** NDI out viewed in Studio Monitor or OBS, including sound/picture sync and the see-through words; a real NDI camera; the CPU cost of NDI out at 1080p on an old PC; the new Settings rows by eye.
- **NDI alone is enough to Go Live** (Jake: "whatever is best long term"): with NDI out on and no destinations switched on, Go Live runs the scenes and NDI out and makes the recording, for a church that streams from OBS. It says so ("Live over NDI only"). Not verified by a person.
