### November 17, 2026 — Livestream on Mac and Linux (branch `ls-macos-linux`)

- **Design:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-17-livestream-mac-linux-design.md`. No new native code; one new crate entry (`security-framework`, macOS only, already in the lock file).
- **Built (with tests of the pure parts, run on Windows):**
  - **The computer's sound in the stream:** Linux reads the chosen output's PulseAudio/PipeWire monitor (`<sink>.monitor`, the default output `@DEFAULT_MONITOR@`) through `board_feed`, so gap filling, Mute and the meter are the same code as the sound board. macOS reads a loopback device (BlackHole) as a sound input (`record::devices::mac_loopback`); without one the stream goes out without the PC's sound, and Settings → Livestream says what to install (new text `stream::tr::mac_needs_loopback`, shown only on a Mac). `pc_sound::start` now takes the FFmpeg path (one-line change in `record::run`).
  - **Sound inputs on Linux:** read by FFmpeg's `pulse`, or by `parec` when that FFmpeg was built without PulseAudio (the static FFmpeg the app downloads may be); listed by FFmpeg, else `pactl list sources`.
  - **Cameras on Linux:** from `/sys/class/video4linux` (only each camera's picture device), saved by their lasting `/dev/v4l/by-id/…` name, labelled readably on the scene buttons (`record::devices`).
  - **Sound outputs:** libmpv lists each output under several drivers; Linux keeps PipeWire (else PulseAudio) names, a Mac Core Audio (`video::ffi::audio_devices_from`).
  - **Stream keys and sign-ins:** macOS Keychain; Linux the desktop's password store over D-Bus (Secret Service via `zbus`, already used on Linux), else in memory until the app closes (the old non-Windows fallback forgot keys at once; fixed).
  - **Encoder:** "The Mac's own" (`h264_videotoolbox`) added to Settings (at the end, so saved choices keep their place) and used when x264 is missing.
  - **NDI runtime:** also looked for where the Mac and Linux installers put it.
  - **Mac packaging:** camera and microphone words in `packaging/macos/Info.plist` (Packager.toml `infoPlistPath`) and the hardened runtime's camera and audio-input entitlements.
- **Checked here:** Windows `cargo check` clean; tests `record:: stream:: video::ffi settings`: 356 passed, 1 failed under load (`hold_keeps_the_picture_until_it_is_let_go`, passed when run alone; it records no sound). Cross `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`: stops in C build scripts (fontconfig needs pkg-config, then `ring` needs a C cross compiler), so the app's Linux/macOS-only code was not compiled as a whole. The key stores (Keychain and Secret Service) were compiled for both `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` and `aarch64-apple-darwin` in a small stand-alone crate: clean.
- **Needs a real Linux PC:** the app compiling at all; the PC's sound reaching the stream (PipeWire and PulseAudio); `parec` with a static FFmpeg; cameras listed and opened (v4l2 in the downloaded FFmpeg); keys kept in GNOME Keyring/KWallet, including a locked store's unlock prompt (it waits on the screen thread); NDI from `libndi.so.6`. The `.deb` might list `pulseaudio-utils` (for `parec`/`pactl`) as recommended.
- **Needs a real Mac:** the app compiling; BlackHole read as the PC's sound with a Multi-Output Device; the camera/microphone permission prompts in a packaged app (FFmpeg as the app's child); VideoToolbox encoding; Keychain prompts with an ad-hoc signature; NDI from `/usr/local/lib/libndi.dylib`. Later, a Core Audio tap (macOS 14.2+) could capture the PC's sound without BlackHole: that needs native code built on a Mac.
- **Not touched:** auto-follow's own listening (`follow::audio`) still reads Linux sound with FFmpeg's `pulse` only; it could use `record::devices::sound_reader` too.

### November 17, 2026 — NDI extras (branch `ls-ndi-extras`)

Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-17-ndi-extras-design.md`.

- **NDI cameras that move.** `stream::ptz::Control` is now `Visca { host, port }` or `Ndi(source)`; `Control::of(camera, address)`: a control address in Settings wins, otherwise an NDI camera is moved over NDI (no address needed). The operator's positions while live, macro steps and slide actions all go through it. Position n is the camera's NDI preset n − 1 (as VISCA), full speed. While live the camera's feed carries the call (`ndi::Remote`, kept by source name); otherwise a short metadata-only connection waits up to 5 s for the camera, calls the position back and closes. The operator's position list shows for an NDI camera only once it says it can move (`NDIlib_recv_ptz_is_supported`); it is looked at again with the scene button. Macro lists offer an NDI camera's positions whether or not it can move (only known once connected).
- **NDI camera tally.** `ndi::show_tally(cameras, &Tally)` (called from the operator's scene button, 4× a second) sets each received NDI camera's on-air / next light with `NDIlib_recv_set_tally`, read only through `Tally::light`, so the Program/Preview meaning of "next" carries over. Sent on change and every 2 s; off when the stream stops (the receivers close).
- **Words alone in a file.** Settings → Media → Livestream → "Words alone" (off by default): while live, `<recording name> words.mov` beside the recording (`stream::words_file`): QuickTime Animation (`qtrle`, `argb`, straight alpha) in MOV, a full picture every 10 s; small because only changes are stored. Fed from its own thread and FFmpeg (wallclock timestamps, `-fps_mode cfr`): the writer hands over only changed words, so the recording and stream never wait for it. Finished beside the recording at Stop. Lost if the app crashes (MOV is only readable once finished).
- **Only NDI SDK calls declared:** `NDIlib_recv_set_tally`, `NDIlib_recv_ptz_is_supported`, `NDIlib_recv_ptz_recall_preset` (plus, in tests only, the sender-side `send_add_connection_metadata`, `send_capture`, `send_free_metadata`, `send_get_tally`).
- **Tests:** `an_ndi_camera_is_moved_and_told_its_tally` (real NDI runtime: a stand-in PTZ source sees the preset recall and its tally, while live and not), `an_ndi_camera_is_moved_over_ndi_unless_it_has_a_control_address`, `the_words_alone_are_recorded_see_through_around_them` (real FFmpeg/ffprobe), `the_words_file_sits_beside_the_recording_and_never_over_another`, `go_live_records_the_words_alone_beside_the_recording`, `settings_keep_the_words_alone_file`.
- **Not verified by a person:** a real NDI PTZ camera (some makers may count presets from 1), a real camera's tally light, the words file opened in Premiere / Final Cut / DaVinci Resolve, the new Settings row by eye. Texts not translated yet (the lead agent runs the extractor).

### November 17, 2026 — Own scenes (branch `ls-scenes`)

- Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-17-own-scenes-design.md`.
- **Built (with tests; new texts not translated, i18n extractor not run):**
  - `stream::own_scenes`: a church scene is a name and layers, back to front. A layer shows the camera on air, camera 1–4, the room screen, the words, a picture file, a colour or some words of the church's own. It goes in the whole picture, a corner (three sizes), the left or right half, or a box set in percent, and can be see-through. `Prepared` makes a scene ready for one picture size once (places, pictures, colours, text drawn with their see-through); `compose` then puts each frame together. A camera with no picture shows the room screen in its place. Cameras and the room fill their place, cut off equally at the sides; words and pictures fit inside. Layers from a newer version load as "Unknown" and are not drawn.
  - `Scene::Own(Uuid)` (saved as `{"own": "<id>"}`; built-in scenes save as before). `RecordPrefs::own_scenes` (left out when empty, so old settings load). Each scene can stand in for one built-in scene in the automatic choice ("Use it by itself in place of"). Only one scene per built-in scene. A scene chosen by hand is never replaced. A scene deleted after it was chosen shows the room screen.
  - The scene worker (`stream::pictures`) prepares the chosen scene only when it changes and draws the words when the scene has a words layer. `compose::Inputs` gains `cameras` and `own`. The writer passes every camera's picture to the `Mixer` while an own scene is out. The Mixer puts the picture together again only when something the scene shows changes.
  - Settings → Media → Livestream → "Your scenes…" (it saves other changes first, after asking). The dialog `ui/dialogs/stream-scenes.slint` + `src/ui/dialogs/stream_scenes.rs` lets the church add, copy, rename, delete and reorder scenes. It also lets them add, remove and move layers (in front/behind), and set what each layer shows, its place, its box and how see-through it is. The picture is drawn by the same `prepare`/`compose` at 640×360, with stand-ins: grey "Camera N", a blue "Room screen" and sample words. Save, or Cancel (which asks first when something changed); both go back to Settings → Media. Saving Settings keeps the scenes as saved.
  - Own scenes are listed after the built-in ones on the scene button while live, and in slide actions and the macro editor's "Livestream: …" step, by id (renaming keeps the steps). The phone tally counts a camera in an own scene (`own_scenes::shows_camera`).
- Small edits in shared files: `src/record/run.rs` (Mixer fields and update, one line in the writer loop), `src/ui/operator/record.rs` (scene list, tally line, `own_scenes` field), `src/record/run_tests.rs` (`own: vec![]` in `look`, one Mixer test).
- **Tests:** about 30 new (own_scenes, pictures, Mixer, editor, dialog in the window, Settings, scene button, macro step and slide action). With every app test run once at the end, 3338 passed and 6 failed. All 6 failures were real-FFmpeg tests in `record::run` (camera not yet seen, a missing file, sound length) while other agents were building at the same time. `record::run` run alone: 23 passed, 0 failed.
- **Not verified by a person:** the dialog by eye (layout, the 320 px picture, the sliders), a real camera and stream with an own scene, and how fast it is at 1080p on an old PC (half-size or box cameras are scaled every frame, nearest pixel). Not built: dragging layers on the picture, crops, colour keys, clock or video layers, tall-stream scenes. A camera layer for camera 3 or 4 shows the room screen until the Program/Preview branch adds those cameras.

### November 17, 2026 — Program/Preview (branch `ls-preview`)

- Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-17-program-preview-design.md` (livestreaming design §4 item 4).
- **Cameras box** (`ui/operator/cameras.slint`, global `CameraSwitch`; in the live column above the automation strip) while live with a camera: a small "Live" picture (the stream as it is sent) and "Next" (the camera chosen to go out next), refreshed four times a second; "No picture" when there is none. **Switch** (cut) and **Fade** (the scenes' half-second fade) put the next camera out; the one that was out becomes next, so pressing again swaps back. Three or four cameras: a button per camera chooses next (the one out is red and cannot be chosen). A PTZ camera chosen as next shows Position 1–6 to set up its shot first. One camera: the Live picture only. No keyboard shortcuts. The scene button's camera rows still switch at once (and swap with next the same way).
- **Writer** (`record::run`): `Controls::next` and `Controls::fade`; `Recording::switch_camera(i, fade)`, `set_next`, `peek`. The camera feeds keep their places (`Vec<Option<Feed>>`). Every 250 ms the writer shrinks the picture it wrote and the next camera's newest to 320 wide (`record::peek::shrink`, four samples a pixel); the operator's 250 ms ticker turns them into images only when they changed.
- **Up to four cameras**: Settings → Media → Livestream "Third camera", "Fourth camera" (same kinds and control address as the first two); `RecordPrefs::camera3/4(_control)`, `RecordPrefs::cameras()` (in order, stops at the first empty or repeated). PTZ slide actions/macros (`ptz::moves`, `control_of`) cover cameras 3 and 4.
- **Tally**: green is now the camera chosen as next (`Tally::of(live, camera, chosen_next, shows_camera)`); one camera still goes green while the scene shows none.
- Tests: `record::peek` (2), `the_cameras_are_taken_in_order_up_to_four_each_once`, `a_camera_switch_can_fade_from_the_camera_before`, `go_live_shows_small_live_and_next_pictures_and_fades_to_the_next_camera` (real FFmpeg, two stand-in cameras), `green_is_the_camera_chosen_to_go_out_next`, operator: `switch_and_fade_put_the_next_camera_out_and_the_one_out_becomes_next`, `with_more_cameras_any_but_the_one_out_can_be_chosen_next`, `one_camera_shows_the_live_picture_only`, `the_small_pictures_are_shown_and_no_picture_is_said`, `the_next_camera_can_be_moved_before_it_goes_out`, `settings_keep_a_third_and_fourth_camera_with_their_control_addresses`.
- Test runs: `ui::operator::record record:: tally ptz` 134 passed, 1 failed (`go_live_shows_the_camera_with_black_bars`, passed alone). Every app test: 3324 passed, 4 failed, all real-FFmpeg timing tests in `record::run` (`the_apps_sound_is_mixed_in…`, `hold_keeps_the_picture…`, `go_live_switches_scene_while_live`, `go_live_switches_between_two_cameras`); `record::run` alone then failed two others (`a_camera_that_stops…`, `go_live_switches_scene…`); all five passed when run alone. Three other agents were building and testing on the same PC at the time; the same flakiness as on November 15–16.
- New texts are not translated yet (the lead agent runs the extractor after merging).
- **Not verified by a person:** the Cameras box by eye (size in the live column, at 360 px and on a small laptop screen), real cameras, the fade's look, the phone tally with three or four cameras, the new Settings rows by eye. The operator's tally update itself is not driven by a test (it needs a running recording); `Tally::of` is.

### November 17, 2026 — Claude (merged: Program/Preview, own scenes, NDI extras, Mac/Linux; branch `follow-sunday`)

- **What happened:** Jake: "use as many agents as you can to finish building". Four agents built in parallel on branches `ls-preview`, `ls-scenes`, `ls-ndi-extras` and `ls-macos-linux` (their own handoffs are above), then all four were merged here. Fixes after the merge: own scenes get the camera pictures from the new camera list, the NDI tally test uses the new `Tally::of`, and a camera's control address alone moves it again (as before; a slide-action test caught it).
- **Texts:** the 78 new texts were translated into all 13 languages by four agents (drafts, not reviewed by native speakers). Words they were unsure of: Mac menu names (Audio MIDI Setup, Multi-Output Device), "In front"/"Behind", "Use it by itself in place of", and in Swahili "Mandhari" is both scene and theme.
- **Tests:** every app test once on the merged code: 3366 passed, 4 failed. Three passed when run again (one was the Spanish catalog checked while it was still being translated; one the PTZ fix above). `go_live_switches_scene_while_live` fails when all of `record::run` runs at once (the camera shows a moment after 2 s on a busy PC) and passes alone, twice: the same load timing noted on November 15–16. Every test in `tests/` passed.
- **Disk:** C: ran low again during the parallel builds; the quick build's `incremental` cache and 44 old test builds were deleted (they rebuild).
- **Not verified by a person:** everything in the four handoffs above: the Cameras box and new Settings rows, the scenes window, real NDI moving cameras and tally lights, the words file in video editors, and the whole Mac/Linux part on a real Mac and Linux PC.
- **Still to do:** try it (`try-latest.cmd follow-sunday`); a Mac and a Linux PC to build and try on; the smaller "not built" items in the handoffs above (dragging layers, crops, colour keys, clock/video layers, tall-stream scenes, capturing a Mac's sound without BlackHole).

### November 17, 2026 (later) — Livestream scenes: the rest (branch `ls2-scenes`)

- Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-17-scene-extras-design.md`.
- **Built (with tests; new texts not translated, i18n extractor not run):**
  - **Drag on the picture** (Your scenes…): a press chooses the layer in front there, dragging moves it, its corner square resizes it; it becomes a box in whole percent, kept inside the picture. Picture files are read once and kept, so the picture redraws quickly while dragging.
  - **Crop** ("Cut off the edges", per side in percent) for cameras, the room, the words, pictures, videos and web pages.
  - **Colour key** (green screen: colour, how close, soft edge; Cb/Cr distance, so shadows on the screen still go) and **brightness key** (dark or light parts) for cameras, pictures and videos (`stream::key_out`, one table look-up per pixel of the layer's place).
  - **New layers:** clock (seconds, 24-hour), countdown (to a typed time or the service's start time), each with a size and colour (text layers too); **video** (looping, no sound, its own FFmpeg via `camera_feed` at the stream's size and frame rate); **web page** (see below). Clocks are redrawn by the scene worker only when their words change (`Prepared::retick`).
  - **Transitions** (`stream::transition`): cut, fade, slide, stinger (FFmpeg started ahead and blocked on its output, so it plays at once; the scene changes at its middle frame; WebM read with `libvpx-vp9` to keep its see-through). Per scene ("When this scene comes in") with a default ("Changing scenes", `RecordPrefs::scene_transition`, saved with the scenes). The camera Fade button still fades.
  - **Tall scenes** (`stream::tall`): "Wide picture / Tall picture (phones)" in the scenes window; "Place the layers for phones" gives a scene its own tall places. Only with a tall destination and a tall scene does the app put the tall picture together (the scene's tall places, else the wide picture fitted); FFmpeg then reads it as raw RGBA from a local port fed at the frame rate by a task of its own. Otherwise FFmpeg fits the wide picture as before.
  - **Keys for scenes:** Settings → Help lists "Livestream scene: …" for every built-in and own scene, no key by default (`shortcuts::Action::Scene`, stored `scene:camera_words` / `scene:<id>`); a key works anywhere a clear key does and chooses the scene as the scene button does; not live: "Scene keys work while the stream is live."
  - **Browser source:** the app has no webview any more (the `webview`/`edge` data folders are leftovers of the old Tauri app, no crate in Cargo.lock, and `tests/cleanup.rs` forbids it). Built instead with the computer's own Microsoft Edge (or Chrome/Chromium) run headless, its DevTools screencast giving PNG pictures, decoded on a thread of its own (`stream::web_page`, a small WebSocket client, no new crate). Note: Edge's renderer crashed with `--default-background-color`, so the clear background is set over DevTools.
- Small edits in shared files: `src/record/run.rs` (Mixer: transitions, media, tall picture; the writer starts the tall feed and the scene media), `src/record/mod.rs` (`scene_transition` at the end of `RecordPrefs`), `src/ui/operator/record.rs` (`Look::more`, `key_scene`), `src/ui/operator/keys.rs`, `src/ui/operator/file_input.rs` (`Filter::Videos`), `src/ui/dialogs/settings_keys.rs`.
- **Tests:** about 40 new (every app test once at the end: 3406 passed, 2 failed, both real-FFmpeg timing tests in `record::run` that passed when run again alone), including real FFmpeg (a looping video layer, a stinger with see-through counted and played wide and tall, a go-live tall scene received by a stand-in server at 720×1280 with the scene's own tall places) and a real Edge test (a page shown as RGBA).
- **Not verified by a person:** the scenes window by eye (it is now long on the right; the drag frame and corner square), a real green screen, the look of the slide and of a real stinger (and a WebM stinger with see-through on the downloaded FFmpeg, if it lacks `libvpx-vp9` the see-through is lost), speed at 1080p 30 fps on an old PC with keyed cameras, videos and a tall scene together (a tall scene adds a second picture to put together and an extra 250 MB/s of raw picture to FFmpeg over the local port), a web page layer on a PC with a weak graphics card, scene keys in daily use.
